# Weekly session cards — V3 review draft

Ready for review, not approved. All loads and times are planned, not performed. Barbell loads are total pounds; dip/pull-up loads are ADDED pounds (system load = your morning bodyweight + added). Ramps and power sets never count as work sets. One left+right pair of a one-arm/one-leg exercise = one set; time always includes both sides. Strength sessions: 75-minute hard limit, 5-minute delay reserve included. Wednesday impact: 15-minute hard limit. Friday impact: ~30-minute target. Travel is not budgeted. See the [training block](SYNTHESIZED_TRAINING_BLOCK_V3.md) and [verification](SYNTHESIS_VERIFICATION_V3.md).

## Week 1 — Accumulation start (starts Sun Sep 27, 2026)

### Week 1 — Sunday, Sep 27, 2026 (Accumulation start)

**Objective:** Fresh dip priority, paused bench, one-arm chest-supported rows; biceps for size. Starts ≥6 h after the long ride ends.

**No impact today.** Start at least 6 hours after the long ride actually ends.

| # | Exercise | Sets × reps | Load / how to choose it | Reserve | Rest | Purpose | Cut |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Plyometric push-up (hands leave the floor) | 4×3 | Bodyweight. Hands leave the floor by a few inches; land softly and rebound straight into the next rep. | fast intent, RPE ≤4 | 60 s | Upper-body pushing speed for dips and bench; no leg demand after the long ride | cut first (keep at least 2) |
| 2 | Weighted dip heavy double | 1×2 | +45 lb added (≈215 lb system at 170 BW) | 2–3 RIR | 150 s | Heavy specific practice before pressing fatigue | never cut |
| 3 | Weighted dip back-offs | 3×6 | +35 lb added (≈205 lb system at 170 BW) | 2–3 RIR | 150 s | Rep capacity toward six-rep target | never cut |
| 4 | Paused bench press | 3×3 | 180 lb | 2–3 RIR | 150 s | Secondary bench maintenance; controlled one-second pause | cut second (keep at least 2) |
| 5 | One-arm chest-supported dumbbell row (incline bench) | 4×8 /side | First set picks a dumbbell for 8 strict reps at 2–3 RIR; after two comparable exposures at ≥3 RIR, go up one dumbbell step. | 2–3 RIR | 75 s after each L+R pair | Horizontal pull with the chest supported, sparing the back after the long ride | never cut |
| 6 | Alternating dumbbell curl | 3×8–15 /side | First set picks a load for ≥8 clean reps at target reserve; afterwards compare with the last normal appearance of this exact exercise. | 1–2 RIR (last set may reach 0–1) | ≈88 s (superset) | Direct biceps hypertrophy; not counted toward compound pressing/pulling | cut first (keep at least 2) |
| 7 | Cable external rotation | 2×12 /side | Lightest cable setting; elbow at the side. | 4–5 RIR | ≈130 s (superset) | Shoulder health (rotator cuff) | never cut |
| 8 | Ab wheel | 2×8 | Bodyweight; shorten the range or lever to meet reserve. | 3 RIR | 60 s | Direct abdominal work; stable three-week block | never cut |
| 9 | Farmer carry | 2×20 m | Heaviest bells that allow another 20 m with unchanged posture. | easy; no posture loss | 60 s | Grip and trunk stiffness; suitcase = anti-lateral flexion, not rotation | never cut |

**Strength clock** (every block counted once; m:ss):
| Start–end | Block | What happens | Seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0:00–4:00 | Warm-up | Brisk walk 60 s; Thoracic rotations 6/side; Scapular depressions 5 × 3 s; Wall slides 8; Incline push-up rehearsal 5 smooth reps; Unloaded hip hinge 5. Preparation only: no work-set or accessory credit. Wall slides live here and nowhere else. | 180 execution + 30 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 15 rounding = 240 |
| 4:00–10:00 | Daily power | One easy rehearsal set of 2, 30 s, then work sets with 60 s between; 45 s before the dip ramps. | 36 execution + 180 rest + 20 setup and load changes + 20 transition + 15 rehearsal + 30 rehearsal rest + 45 recovery before next + 14 rounding = 360 |
| 10:00–16:00 | Dip ramps | BW×5 → 45 s; +15×3 → 75 s; +30×1 → 120 s. Each rest follows that ramp, including the last rest before the first work set. Ramps never count as work sets. | 42 execution + 240 rest + 60 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 3 rounding = 360 |
| 16:00–26:00 | Dip work (fresh priority) | Full rest between every work set (2:00–3:00 upper, 2:30–3:00 lower). No accessory work during priority rests. | 87 execution + 450 rest + 15 setup and load changes + 20 transition + 28 rounding = 600 |
| 26:00–34:00 | Bench ramps | 45×8 → 30 s; 95×5 → 60 s; 135×3 → 90 s; 165×1 → 120 s. Each rest follows that ramp, including the last rest before the first work set. Ramps never count as work sets. | 74 execution + 300 rest + 75 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 16 rounding = 480 |
| 34:00–40:30 | Paused bench work | Full rest between every work set (2:00–3:00 upper, 2:30–3:00 lower). No accessory work during priority rests. | 45 execution + 300 rest + 20 transition + 25 rounding = 390 |

No extra rest needed: each exercise rests while its partner works (≥60 s). | 366 execution + 50 side changes + 60 station moves + 45 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 4 rounding = 540 |
| 60:00–63:00 | Core | 60 s between sets. | 48 execution + 60 rest + 30 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 27 rounding = 180 |

**Planned strength total: 71:00 (71 min) including the 5-minute delay reserve — limit 75:00.** If every OHP/dip/pull-up/bench rest runs to 3:00, add 2:30 → 73:30 (still inside 75:00).

**Fallbacks:**
- **Plyometric push-up (hands leave the floor):** Hands-elevated plyometric push-up on a bench, same sets and reps.
- **One-arm chest-supported dumbbell row (incline bench):** One-arm chest-supported machine row or one-arm seated cable row, same dose.
- **Alternating dumbbell curl:** Any familiar curl with the same implement type, same dose.
- **Cable external rotation:** Band external rotation, same dose.
- **Ab wheel:** Kneeling short-range rollout.
- **Farmer carry:** Dumbbells instead of kettlebells, same distance.

**Load rules:** All main-lift loads are conditional. If the first work set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, deadlift −10 lb for the remaining sets; if ≥2 RIR harder, double that and drop the last back-off. Progress only after the previous comparable session hit its target RIR with normal technique, bar speed and tissue response. Never catch up a missed increase; change one variable at a time. Dips and pull-ups: log added load AND total system load (morning bodyweight + added). Examples use 170 lb; use your real weight.

**Rest:** OHP, dip, pull-up and bench work sets 2:00 minimum, up to 3:00 when reps or bar speed would suffer (clock budgets 2:30). Squat/deadlift 2:30–3:00 (clock budgets 3:00). Power 60–90 s. Accessories 60–75 s. Supersets pair unrelated muscles and never include priority work sets.

**Power rules:** Power is fast and low-fatigue, never a conditioning circuit. Stop a set at the first visibly slower rep, a noisy catch or landing, or any technique breakdown. After TWO crisp, symptom-free exposures that did not slow the next main-lift ramp, change ONE thing: the next load step, or (only if load cannot rise) a little more height/distance. Never progress power in the same week as a new plyo stage or a new leg-accessory load. W6/W12 use half the sets at a familiar load. Power sets never count as strength sets.

**Power progression (Plyometric push-up (hands leave the floor)):** After two crisp exposures: more flight height, then a clap, then a light vest (+5–10 lb) — one change at a time.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Sunday checks: power follows the long ride — if the push-ups/chest passes lose pop, use the hands-elevated regression and stop at 2 sets. Dips are the fresh priority; nothing tiring comes before them.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd biceps set, then (3) the 3rd bench set (press stays ≥16). Never cut: dip work, row sets, both core sets, cable ER, the carry. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.

**Fallbacks:**
- **Two-hand kettlebell swing:** Light two-hand KB deadlift with a fast stand, same sets × 3; technique fallback, not a ballistic equivalent.

**Load rules:** All main-lift loads are conditional. If the first work set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, deadlift −10 lb for the remaining sets; if ≥2 RIR harder, double that and drop the last back-off. Progress only after the previous comparable session hit its target RIR with normal technique, bar speed and tissue response. Never catch up a missed increase; change one variable at a time. Dips and pull-ups: log added load AND total system load (morning bodyweight + added). Examples use 170 lb; use your real weight.

**Rest:** OHP, dip, pull-up and bench work sets 2:00 minimum, up to 3:00 when reps or bar speed would suffer (clock budgets 2:30). Squat/deadlift 2:30–3:00 (clock budgets 3:00). Power 60–90 s. Accessories 60–75 s. Supersets pair unrelated muscles and never include priority work sets.

**Power rules:** Power is fast and low-fatigue, never a conditioning circuit. Stop a set at the first visibly slower rep, a noisy catch or landing, or any technique breakdown. After TWO crisp, symptom-free exposures that did not slow the next main-lift ramp, change ONE thing: the next load step, or (only if load cannot rise) a little more height/distance. Never progress power in the same week as a new plyo stage or a new leg-accessory load. W6/W12 use half the sets at a familiar load. Power sets never count as strength sets.

**Power progression (Two-hand kettlebell swing):** After two crisp exposures: next bell size (4 kg step). Weeks 7–11 may move to the one-arm snatch if its gate is met.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Calf rules:** Calves: full range, 1-s pause in the stretched position, no bouncing, 3 RIR (week 1: 3–4 RIR while choosing loads). When every set reaches the top of the range with normal next-morning calf/Achilles response AND normal Wednesday pogo quality, add the smallest load step. More than mild calf soreness on Wednesday morning: hold calf load and start Wednesday impact at the lower pogo dose. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR.

**Leg rules:** Extra leg work is never cut: under fatigue lighten the load, shorten the range or add hand support instead. Two work sets per side, 3–4 RIR. Increase the smallest load step only after two comparable exposures at ≥3 RIR with normal adductor/hamstring response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts. Do not raise it in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 2×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Monday checks: swings/snatches come before deadlifts — if the deadlift ramps feel slow, stop power at 2 sets next time. Lunges come after a Sunday long ride and deadlifts — after a hard ride weekend, hold the lunge load.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd triceps set, (3) 3rd calf set. Never cut: pull-up, deadlift and OHP work sets, both lunge sets, Copenhagen, both Pallof sets. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.


### Week 1 — Wednesday, Sep 30, 2026 (Accumulation start)

**Objective:** Impact first (≤15 min). Fresh heavy OHP priority, pull-ups, one-arm rows; biceps for size.

**Impact session — 15-minute hard limit; travel is not budgeted.** Planned: 6:43.

| Order | Activity | Dose and rest | Seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Preparation (general warm-up, counted here once) | 60 s walk; ankle rocks 8/side; calf raises 8; march 10 steps/side; 5 quarter-squats; readiness check | 180 |
| 2 | Pogo jumps (bilateral) | 4×10; 20 s between sets | 115 |
| 3 | Low-hurdle hops (15–20 cm, bilateral) | 2×6; 30 s between sets; 30 s setup | 78 |
| 4 | Transitions | Moving between drills, markers and measuring | 30 |

**Contacts:** low 40, moderate 12, high 0.

Plyo and sprints come before lifting, never after a hard ride or tiring lifting. Progress only after two tolerated exposures with normal next mornings. If today is cut short, log the actual contacts and metres; a cut session never qualifies the next stage. No make-up impact after lifting or on Saturday. Contact counting: each ground contact = 1; a bilateral landing = 1; per-leg work counts per leg (none prescribed).

**Tissue check:** After every impact session and the next morning, log normal/abnormal for adductors, calves and Achilles. If abnormal: location, severity, onset, duration, stride/gait effect, squat effect and response to gentle resisted adduction. Familiar mild soreness that clears in 24–48 h with no movement change: HOLD (repeat the stage). Worsening, persistent or output-limiting symptoms: regress. Sharp pain, bruising, weakness, progressive symptoms or ANY movement-altering discomfort: stop the affected impact work and get a clinical evaluation. No aggressive static stretching before sprints.

**OHP anchor:** your strict 125×1 at RPE 8.5 on Sunday, Sep 20, 2026 replaces the old week-1 calibration single (estimated max ≈130–133). Today’s 117.5 top double should feel about RPE 7.5–8. If it is RPE ≥9, drop the back-offs to 110 and repeat 117.5 next week; if ≤7, still take the printed path (no jump ahead).

| 60:00–64:00 | Carry | 60 s between sets (each set = left then right). | 100 execution + 60 rest + 20 side changes + 30 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 15 rounding = 240 |
| 64:00–69:00 | Delay reserve | Unallocated time for crowded equipment, longer priority rests (up to 3:00) or logging. Never filled with extra sets. | 300 delay reserve = 300 |

**Planned strength total: 69:00 (69 min) including the 5-minute delay reserve — limit 75:00.** If every OHP/dip/pull-up/bench rest runs to 3:00, add 3:00 → 72:00 (still inside 75:00).

**Fallbacks:**
- **Rotational medicine-ball scoop throw (to a wall):** Landmine rotational punch, same sets × reps per side, light; still counts as dynamic rotation. Use it if there is no ball or wall space.
- **One-arm dumbbell row (bench-supported):** One-arm chest-supported machine row or one-arm seated cable row, same dose.
- **Straight-bar cable curl:** Any familiar curl with the same implement type, same dose.
- **Face pull:** Band face pull or reverse fly, same dose.
- **Hanging leg raise:** Bent-knee hanging raise.
- **Suitcase carry:** Dumbbells instead of kettlebells, same distance.

**Load rules:** All main-lift loads are conditional. If the first work set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, deadlift −10 lb for the remaining sets; if ≥2 RIR harder, double that and drop the last back-off. Progress only after the previous comparable session hit its target RIR with normal technique, bar speed and tissue response. Never catch up a missed increase; change one variable at a time. Dips and pull-ups: log added load AND total system load (morning bodyweight + added). Examples use 170 lb; use your real weight.

**Rest:** OHP, dip, pull-up and bench work sets 2:00 minimum, up to 3:00 when reps or bar speed would suffer (clock budgets 2:30). Squat/deadlift 2:30–3:00 (clock budgets 3:00). Power 60–90 s. Accessories 60–75 s. Supersets pair unrelated muscles and never include priority work sets.

**Power rules:** Power is fast and low-fatigue, never a conditioning circuit. Stop a set at the first visibly slower rep, a noisy catch or landing, or any technique breakdown. After TWO crisp, symptom-free exposures that did not slow the next main-lift ramp, change ONE thing: the next load step, or (only if load cannot rise) a little more height/distance. Never progress power in the same week as a new plyo stage or a new leg-accessory load. W6/W12 use half the sets at a familiar load. Power sets never count as strength sets.

**Power progression (Rotational medicine-ball scoop throw (to a wall)):** After two crisp exposures: +1–2 kg ball.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Wednesday checks: scoop throws precede heavy OHP — keep throws at RPE ≤4 and stop at any shoulder fatigue. Monday calves are 48 h before today’s pogos — see the calf rule.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power pairs 3–4, (2) 3rd biceps set. Never cut: OHP and pull-up work sets, row sets, face pulls, both core sets, the carry. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.


### Week 1 — Friday, Oct 2, 2026 (Accumulation start)

**Objective:** Impact and sprints first. Low-bar squat maintenance, dips, DB incline bench, pull-ups; single-leg RDL, Copenhagen, calves and triceps.

**Impact session — 30-minute target (not a hard limit); travel is not budgeted.** Planned: 15:55.

| Order | Activity | Dose and rest | Seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Preparation (general warm-up, counted here once) | 60 s walk; ankle rocks 8/side; calf raises 8; march 10 steps/side; 5 quarter-squats; readiness check | 180 |
| 2 | Forward-back line hops (bilateral) | 2×10; 20 s between sets | 55 |
| 3 | Submaximal broad jumps (~75%) in doubles | 4 sets × 2 jumps (jump, reset, jump); 30 s between sets | 137 |
| 4 | Gentle pre-sprint adductor squeeze | 3×20 s, 20 s between; ~20–30% effort | 100 |
| 5 | Sprint setup | Measured route, clear runout and a gradual stop; no extra accelerations | 30 |
| 6 | Accelerations with equal-distance easy runout | 4×15 m on ~5% hill, build to ~75%; 120 s after each runout before the next rep | 408 |
| 7 | Transitions | Moving between drills, markers and measuring | 45 |

**Contacts:** low 20, moderate 8, high 0. **Sprints:** 60 m of accelerations + 60 m easy runout.

Plyo and sprints come before lifting, never after a hard ride or tiring lifting. Progress only after two tolerated exposures with normal next mornings. If today is cut short, log the actual contacts and metres; a cut session never qualifies the next stage. No make-up impact after lifting or on Saturday. Contact counting: each ground contact = 1; a bilateral landing = 1; per-leg work counts per leg (none prescribed).

**Tissue check:** After every impact session and the next morning, log normal/abnormal for adductors, calves and Achilles. If abnormal: location, severity, onset, duration, stride/gait effect, squat effect and response to gentle resisted adduction. Familiar mild soreness that clears in 24–48 h with no movement change: HOLD (repeat the stage). Worsening, persistent or output-limiting symptoms: regress. Sharp pain, bruising, weakness, progressive symptoms or ANY movement-altering discomfort: stop the affected impact work and get a clinical evaluation. No aggressive static stretching before sprints.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Calf rules:** Calves: full range, 1-s pause in the stretched position, no bouncing, 3 RIR (week 1: 3–4 RIR while choosing loads). When every set reaches the top of the range with normal next-morning calf/Achilles response AND normal Wednesday pogo quality, add the smallest load step. More than mild calf soreness on Wednesday morning: hold calf load and start Wednesday impact at the lower pogo dose. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR.

**Leg rules:** Extra leg work is never cut: under fatigue lighten the load, shorten the range or add hand support instead. Two work sets per side, 3–4 RIR. Increase the smallest load step only after two comparable exposures at ≥3 RIR with normal adductor/hamstring response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts. Do not raise it in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 2×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Friday checks: power comes after impact — if cleans are slow, do 2 sets only. DB incline bench is supersetted with the single-leg RDL — hold incline’s 2nd set first if squat or dip felt heavy. The single-leg RDL itself still follows squats — lighten it if hamstrings or lower back are tired.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd triceps set, (3) 3rd calf set, then (4) skip DB incline bench entirely — both sets, never just one (press drops to 18; the single-leg RDL then runs alone with 75 s between pairs). Never cut: squat, dip, both pull-up sets (vertical floor 8), both single-leg RDL sets, Copenhagen, prone Y, both landmine rotation sets. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Sequencing:** Sunday checks: power follows the long ride — if the push-ups/chest passes lose pop, use the hands-elevated regression and stop at 2 sets. Dips are the fresh priority; nothing tiring comes before them.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd biceps set, then (3) the 3rd bench set (press stays ≥16). Never cut: dip work, row sets, both core sets, cable ER, the carry. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.

**Fallbacks:**
- **Two-hand kettlebell swing:** Light two-hand KB deadlift with a fast stand, same sets × 3; technique fallback, not a ballistic equivalent.
- **Dumbbell/kettlebell reverse lunge:** Split-stance static lunge with hand support, same dose.
- **Knee-supported short-lever Copenhagen:** Side-lying adductor lift with the top leg supported, same sets and reps.
- **One-leg dumbbell calf raise on a step:** Standing Smith-machine calf raise on a plate, 3×8–15.
- **Rope pushdown:** Straight-bar or V-bar pushdown, same dose.
- **Pallof press:** Band Pallof press anchored to a rack, same dose.

**Load rules:** All main-lift loads are conditional. If the first work set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, deadlift −10 lb for the remaining sets; if ≥2 RIR harder, double that and drop the last back-off. Progress only after the previous comparable session hit its target RIR with normal technique, bar speed and tissue response. Never catch up a missed increase; change one variable at a time. Dips and pull-ups: log added load AND total system load (morning bodyweight + added). Examples use 170 lb; use your real weight.

**Rest:** OHP, dip, pull-up and bench work sets 2:00 minimum, up to 3:00 when reps or bar speed would suffer (clock budgets 2:30). Squat/deadlift 2:30–3:00 (clock budgets 3:00). Power 60–90 s. Accessories 60–75 s. Supersets pair unrelated muscles and never include priority work sets.

**Power rules:** Power is fast and low-fatigue, never a conditioning circuit. Stop a set at the first visibly slower rep, a noisy catch or landing, or any technique breakdown. After TWO crisp, symptom-free exposures that did not slow the next main-lift ramp, change ONE thing: the next load step, or (only if load cannot rise) a little more height/distance. Never progress power in the same week as a new plyo stage or a new leg-accessory load. W6/W12 use half the sets at a familiar load. Power sets never count as strength sets.

**Power progression (Two-hand kettlebell swing):** After two crisp exposures: next bell size (4 kg step). Weeks 7–11 may move to the one-arm snatch if its gate is met.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Calf rules:** Calves: full range, 1-s pause in the stretched position, no bouncing, 3 RIR (week 1: 3–4 RIR while choosing loads). When every set reaches the top of the range with normal next-morning calf/Achilles response AND normal Wednesday pogo quality, add the smallest load step. More than mild calf soreness on Wednesday morning: hold calf load and start Wednesday impact at the lower pogo dose. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR.

**Leg rules:** Extra leg work is never cut: under fatigue lighten the load, shorten the range or add hand support instead. Two work sets per side, 3–4 RIR. Increase the smallest load step only after two comparable exposures at ≥3 RIR with normal adductor/hamstring response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts. Do not raise it in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 2×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Monday checks: swings/snatches come before deadlifts — if the deadlift ramps feel slow, stop power at 2 sets next time. Lunges come after a Sunday long ride and deadlifts — after a hard ride weekend, hold the lunge load.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd triceps set, (3) 3rd calf set. Never cut: pull-up, deadlift and OHP work sets, both lunge sets, Copenhagen, both Pallof sets. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.

**Strength clock** (every block counted once; m:ss):
| Start–end | Block | What happens | Seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0:00–5:00 | Warm-up | Thoracic rotations 6/side; Gentle hip rock-back 5/side; Bodyweight squat 5; Wall slides 8; Brace rehearsal 3 × 5 s; KB complex primer 2 untimed rounds: 2 double-KB cleans + 1 press + 2 front squats, light 8–12 kg bells, ~30 s between rounds. General prep (walk, ankle rocks, calf raises, marching, quarter-squats) was done on the impact clock and is not repeated. If impact was skipped, add a 60 s walk and 8 ankle rocks/side from the delay reserve. Preparation only: no work-set or accessory credit. Wall slides live here and nowhere else. | 220 execution + 45 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 20 rounding = 300 |
| 5:00–10:30 | Daily power | The KB complex in the warm-up is the rehearsal. 60 s between sets; 45 s before the next ramps. | 36 execution + 180 rest + 30 setup and load changes + 20 transition + 45 recovery before next + 19 rounding = 330 |
| 10:30–18:00 | Squat ramps | 45×5 → 30 s; 135×3 → 60 s; 185×1 → 90 s; 205×1 → 120 s. Each rest follows that ramp, including the last rest before the first work set. Ramps never count as work sets. | 52 execution + 300 rest + 75 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 8 rounding = 450 |

| 25:30–29:00 | Dip ramps | BW×3 → 45 s; +15×1 → 75 s. Each rest follows that ramp, including the last rest before the first work set. Ramps never count as work sets. | 22 execution + 120 rest + 45 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 8 rounding = 210 |

**Fallbacks:**
- **Double-kettlebell clean (from the hang):** Trap-bar jump, about 95–135 lb, same sets × reps (its landings count as moderate plyo contacts). If no trap bar: two-hand swing 4×5.
- **Dumbbell incline bench press:** Flat dumbbell bench press, same dose, if an incline bench is unavailable.
- **Dumbbell/kettlebell single-leg RDL:** Kickstand (staggered-stance) dumbbell RDL, same dose.
- **Knee-supported short-lever Copenhagen:** Side-lying adductor lift with the top leg supported, same sets and reps.
- **Seated calf raise:** Seated dumbbell-on-knees calf raise, forefoot on a plate, same dose.
- **Overhead cable triceps extension:** Seated two-hand dumbbell overhead extension, same dose. No skull crushers.
- **Prone Y (incline bench):** Prone T/Y on the floor, same dose.
- **Landmine rotation:** High-to-low cable chop, same dose.

**Load rules:** All main-lift loads are conditional. If the first work set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, deadlift −10 lb for the remaining sets; if ≥2 RIR harder, double that and drop the last back-off. Progress only after the previous comparable session hit its target RIR with normal technique, bar speed and tissue response. Never catch up a missed increase; change one variable at a time. Dips and pull-ups: log added load AND total system load (morning bodyweight + added). Examples use 170 lb; use your real weight.

**Rest:** OHP, dip, pull-up and bench work sets 2:00 minimum, up to 3:00 when reps or bar speed would suffer (clock budgets 2:30). Squat/deadlift 2:30–3:00 (clock budgets 3:00). Power 60–90 s. Accessories 60–75 s. Supersets pair unrelated muscles and never include priority work sets.

**Power rules:** Power is fast and low-fatigue, never a conditioning circuit. Stop a set at the first visibly slower rep, a noisy catch or landing, or any technique breakdown. After TWO crisp, symptom-free exposures that did not slow the next main-lift ramp, change ONE thing: the next load step, or (only if load cannot rise) a little more height/distance. Never progress power in the same week as a new plyo stage or a new leg-accessory load. W6/W12 use half the sets at a familiar load. Power sets never count as strength sets.

**Power progression (Double-kettlebell clean (from the hang)):** After two crisp exposures: next bell size for both bells.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Calf rules:** Calves: full range, 1-s pause in the stretched position, no bouncing, 3 RIR (week 1: 3–4 RIR while choosing loads). When every set reaches the top of the range with normal next-morning calf/Achilles response AND normal Wednesday pogo quality, add the smallest load step. More than mild calf soreness on Wednesday morning: hold calf load and start Wednesday impact at the lower pogo dose. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR.

**Leg rules:** Extra leg work is never cut: under fatigue lighten the load, shorten the range or add hand support instead. Two work sets per side, 3–4 RIR. Increase the smallest load step only after two comparable exposures at ≥3 RIR with normal adductor/hamstring response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts. Do not raise it in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 2×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Friday checks: power comes after impact — if cleans are slow, do 2 sets only. DB incline bench is supersetted with the single-leg RDL — hold incline’s 2nd set first if squat or dip felt heavy. The single-leg RDL itself still follows squats — lighten it if hamstrings or lower back are tired.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd triceps set, (3) 3rd calf set, then (4) skip DB incline bench entirely — both sets, never just one (press drops to 18; the single-leg RDL then runs alone with 75 s between pairs). Never cut: squat, dip, both pull-up sets (vertical floor 8), both single-leg RDL sets, Copenhagen, prone Y, both landmine rotation sets. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.

**Rest:** OHP, dip, pull-up and bench work sets 2:00 minimum, up to 3:00 when reps or bar speed would suffer (clock budgets 2:30). Squat/deadlift 2:30–3:00 (clock budgets 3:00). Power 60–90 s. Accessories 60–75 s. Supersets pair unrelated muscles and never include priority work sets.

**Power rules:** Power is fast and low-fatigue, never a conditioning circuit. Stop a set at the first visibly slower rep, a noisy catch or landing, or any technique breakdown. After TWO crisp, symptom-free exposures that did not slow the next main-lift ramp, change ONE thing: the next load step, or (only if load cannot rise) a little more height/distance. Never progress power in the same week as a new plyo stage or a new leg-accessory load. W6/W12 use half the sets at a familiar load. Power sets never count as strength sets.

**Power progression (Two-hand kettlebell swing):** After two crisp exposures: next bell size (4 kg step). Weeks 7–11 may move to the one-arm snatch if its gate is met.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Calf rules:** Calves: full range, 1-s pause in the stretched position, no bouncing, 3 RIR (week 1: 3–4 RIR while choosing loads). When every set reaches the top of the range with normal next-morning calf/Achilles response AND normal Wednesday pogo quality, add the smallest load step. More than mild calf soreness on Wednesday morning: hold calf load and start Wednesday impact at the lower pogo dose. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR.

**Leg rules:** Extra leg work is never cut: under fatigue lighten the load, shorten the range or add hand support instead. Two work sets per side, 3–4 RIR. Increase the smallest load step only after two comparable exposures at ≥3 RIR with normal adductor/hamstring response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts. Do not raise it in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 2×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Monday checks: swings/snatches come before deadlifts — if the deadlift ramps feel slow, stop power at 2 sets next time. Lunges come after a Sunday long ride and deadlifts — after a hard ride weekend, hold the lunge load.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd triceps set, (3) 3rd calf set. Never cut: pull-up, deadlift and OHP work sets, both lunge sets, Copenhagen, both Pallof sets. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.

DB incline bench is supersetted with the single-leg RDL — hold incline’s 2nd set first if squat or dip felt heavy. The single-leg RDL itself still follows squats — lighten it if hamstrings or lower back are tired.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd triceps set, (3) 3rd calf set, then (4) skip DB incline bench entirely — both sets, never just one (press drops to 18; the single-leg RDL then runs alone with 75 s between pairs). Never cut: squat, dip, both pull-up sets (vertical floor 8), both single-leg RDL sets, Copenhagen, prone Y, both landmine rotation sets. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.

**Planned strength total: 70:30 (70.5 min) including the 5-minute delay reserve — limit 75:00.** If every OHP/dip/pull-up/bench rest runs to 3:00, add 2:30 → 73:00 (still inside 75:00).

**Fallbacks:**
- **Medicine-ball chest pass to a wall:** Plyometric push-up, same sets and reps.
- **One-arm chest-supported dumbbell row (incline bench):** One-arm chest-supported machine row or one-arm seated cable row, same dose.
- **Alternating dumbbell curl:** Any familiar curl with the same implement type, same dose.
- **Cable external rotation:** Band external rotation, same dose.
- **Hollow-body hold:** Tucked hollow hold.
- **Farmer carry:** Dumbbells instead of kettlebells, same distance.

**Load rules:** All main-lift loads are conditional. If the first work set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, deadlift −10 lb for the remaining sets; if ≥2 RIR harder, double that and drop the last back-off. Progress only after the previous comparable session hit its target RIR with normal technique, bar speed and tissue response. Never catch up a missed increase; change one variable at a time. Dips and pull-ups: log added load AND total system load (morning bodyweight + added). Examples use 170 lb; use your real weight.

**Rest:** OHP, dip, pull-up and bench work sets 2:00 minimum, up to 3:00 when reps or bar speed would suffer (clock budgets 2:30). Squat/deadlift 2:30–3:00 (clock budgets 3:00). Power 60–90 s. Accessories 60–75 s. Supersets pair unrelated muscles and never include priority work sets.

**Power rules:** Power is fast and low-fatigue, never a conditioning circuit. Stop a set at the first visibly slower rep, a noisy catch or landing, or any technique breakdown. After TWO crisp, symptom-free exposures that did not slow the next main-lift ramp, change ONE thing: the next load step, or (only if load cannot rise) a little more height/distance. Never progress power in the same week as a new plyo stage or a new leg-accessory load. W6/W12 use half the sets at a familiar load. Power sets never count as strength sets.

**Power progression (Medicine-ball chest pass to a wall):** After two crisp exposures: +1–2 kg ball.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Sunday checks: power follows the long ride — if the push-ups/chest passes lose pop, use the hands-elevated regression and stop at 2 sets. Dips are the fresh priority; nothing tiring comes before them.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd biceps set, then (3) the 3rd bench set (press stays ≥16). Never cut: dip work, row sets, both core sets, cable ER, the carry. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.

**Calf rules:** Calves: full range, 1-s pause in the stretched position, no bouncing, 3 RIR (week 1: 3–4 RIR while choosing loads). When every set reaches the top of the range with normal next-morning calf/Achilles response AND normal Wednesday pogo quality, add the smallest load step. **Leg rules:** Extra leg work is never cut: under fatigue lighten the load, shorten the range or add hand support instead. Two work sets per side, 3–4 RIR. Increase the smallest load step only after two comparable exposures at ≥3 RIR with normal adductor/hamstring response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts. Plyo and sprints come before lifting, never after a hard ride or tiring lifting. Progress only after two tolerated exposures with normal next mornings. If today is cut short, log the actual contacts and metres; a cut session never qualifies the next stage. No make-up impact after lifting or on Saturday. Contact counting: each ground contact = 1; a bilateral landing = 1; per-leg work counts per leg (none prescribed).

**Tissue check:** After every impact session and the next morning, log normal/abnormal for adductors, calves and Achilles. If abnormal: location, severity, onset, duration, stride/gait effect, squat effect and response to gentle resisted adduction. Familiar mild soreness that clears in 24–48 h with no movement change: HOLD (repeat the stage). Worsening, persistent or output-limiting symptoms: regress. Sharp pain, bruising, weakness, progressive symptoms or ANY movement-altering discomfort: stop the affected impact work and get a clinical evaluation. No aggressive static stretching before sprints.

| 57:00–60:00 | Core | 60 s between sets. | 48 execution + 60 rest + 30 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 27 rounding = 180 |

**Planned strength total: 69:00 (69 min) including the 5-minute delay reserve — limit 75:00.** If every OHP/dip/pull-up/bench rest runs to 3:00, add 3:00 → 72:00 (still inside 75:00).

**Fallbacks:**
- **Rotational medicine-ball scoop throw (to a wall):** Landmine rotational punch, same sets × reps per side, light; still counts as dynamic rotation. Use it if there is no ball or wall space.
- **One-arm dumbbell row (bench-supported):** One-arm chest-supported machine row or one-arm seated cable row, same dose.
- **Straight-bar cable curl:** Any familiar curl with the same implement type, same dose.
- **Face pull:** Band face pull or reverse fly, same dose.
- **Body saw:** Short-range ab wheel if no sliding surface.
- **Suitcase carry:** Dumbbells instead of kettlebells, same distance.

**Load rules:** All main-lift loads are conditional. If the first work set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, deadlift −10 lb for the remaining sets; if ≥2 RIR harder, double that and drop the last back-off. Progress only after the previous comparable session hit its target RIR with normal technique, bar speed and tissue response. Never catch up a missed increase; change one variable at a time. Dips and pull-ups: log added load AND total system load (morning bodyweight + added). Examples use 170 lb; use your real weight.

**Rest:** OHP, dip, pull-up and bench work sets 2:00 minimum, up to 3:00 when reps or bar speed would suffer (clock budgets 2:30). Squat/deadlift 2:30–3:00 (clock budgets 3:00). Power 60–90 s. Accessories 60–75 s. Supersets pair unrelated muscles and never include priority work sets.

**Power rules:** Power is fast and low-fatigue, never a conditioning circuit. Stop a set at the first visibly slower rep, a noisy catch or landing, or any technique breakdown. After TWO crisp, symptom-free exposures that did not slow the next main-lift ramp, change ONE thing: the next load step, or (only if load cannot rise) a little more height/distance. Never progress power in the same week as a new plyo stage or a new leg-accessory load. W6/W12 use half the sets at a familiar load. Power sets never count as strength sets.

**Power progression (Rotational medicine-ball scoop throw (to a wall)):** After two crisp exposures: +1–2 kg ball.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Wednesday checks: scoop throws precede heavy OHP — keep throws at RPE ≤4 and stop at any shoulder fatigue. Monday calves are 48 h before today’s pogos — see the calf rule.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power pairs 3–4, (2) 3rd biceps set. Never cut: OHP and pull-up work sets, row sets, face pulls, both core sets, the carry. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.


### Week 4 — Friday, Oct 23, 2026 (Accumulation)

**Objective:** Impact and sprints first. Low-bar squat maintenance, dips, DB incline bench, pull-ups; single-leg RDL, Copenhagen, calves and triceps.

**Impact session — 30-minute target (not a hard limit); travel is not budgeted.** Planned: 21:27.

| Order | Activity | Dose and rest | Seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Preparation (general warm-up, counted here once) | 60 s walk; ankle rocks 8/side; calf raises 8; march 10 steps/side; 5 quarter-squats; readiness check | 180 |
| 2 | Forward-back line hops (bilateral) | 3×10; 20 s between sets | 85 |
| 3 | Submaximal broad jumps (~75%) in doubles | 5 sets × 2 jumps (jump, reset, jump); 30 s between sets | 175 |
| 4 | Gentle pre-sprint adductor squeeze | 3×20 s, 20 s between; ~20–30% effort | 100 |
| 5 | Sprint setup | Measured route, clear runout and a gradual stop; no extra accelerations | 30 |
| 6 | Accelerations with equal-distance easy runout | 6×15 m on ~5% hill, build to ~75%; 120 s after each runout before the next rep | 672 |
| 7 | Transitions | Moving between drills, markers and measuring | 45 |

**Contacts:** low 30, moderate 10, high 0. **Sprints:** 90 m of accelerations + 90 m easy runout.

Plyo and sprints come before lifting, never after a hard ride or tiring lifting. Progress only after two tolerated exposures with normal next mornings. If today is cut short, log the actual contacts and metres; a cut session never qualifies the next stage. No make-up impact after lifting or on Saturday. Contact counting: each ground contact = 1; a bilateral landing = 1; per-leg work counts per leg (none prescribed).

**Tissue check:** After every impact session and the next morning, log normal/abnormal for adductors, calves and Achilles. If abnormal: location, severity, onset, duration, stride/gait effect, squat effect and response to gentle resisted adduction. Familiar mild soreness that clears in 24–48 h with no movement change: HOLD (repeat the stage). Worsening, persistent or output-limiting symptoms: regress. Sharp pain, bruising, weakness, progressive symptoms or ANY movement-altering discomfort: stop the affected impact work and get a clinical evaluation. No aggressive static stretching before sprints.

**Load rules:** All main-lift loads are conditional. If the first work set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, deadlift −10 lb for the remaining sets; if ≥2 RIR harder, double that and drop the last back-off. **Power rules:** Power is fast and low-fatigue, never a conditioning circuit. Stop a set at the first visibly slower rep, a noisy catch or landing, or any technique breakdown. After TWO crisp, symptom-free exposures that did not slow the next main-lift ramp, change ONE thing: the next load step, or (only if load cannot rise) a little more height/distance. **Power progression (Double-kettlebell clean (from the hang)):** After two crisp exposures: next bell size for both bells.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Calf rules:** Calves: full range, 1-s pause in the stretched position, no bouncing, 3 RIR (week 1: 3–4 RIR while choosing loads). When every set reaches the top of the range with normal next-morning calf/Achilles response AND normal Wednesday pogo quality, add the smallest load step. More than mild calf soreness on Wednesday morning: hold calf load and start Wednesday impact at the lower pogo dose. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR.

**Leg rules:** Extra leg work is never cut: under fatigue lighten the load, shorten the range or add hand support instead. Two work sets per side, 3–4 RIR. Increase the smallest load step only after two comparable exposures at ≥3 RIR with normal adductor/hamstring response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts. Do not raise it in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 2×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Friday checks: power comes after impact — if cleans are slow, do 2 sets only. DB incline bench is supersetted with the single-leg RDL — hold incline’s 2nd set first if squat or dip felt heavy. The single-leg RDL itself still follows squats — lighten it if hamstrings or lower back are tired.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd triceps set, (3) 3rd calf set, then (4) skip DB incline bench entirely — both sets, never just one (press drops to 18; the single-leg RDL then runs alone with 75 s between pairs). Never cut: squat, dip, both pull-up sets (vertical floor 8), both single-leg RDL sets, Copenhagen, prone Y, both landmine rotation sets. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. **Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Sunday checks: power follows the long ride — if the push-ups/chest passes lose pop, use the hands-elevated regression and stop at 2 sets. Dips are the fresh priority; nothing tiring comes before them.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd biceps set, then (3) the 3rd bench set (press stays ≥16). Never cut: dip work, row sets, both core sets, cable ER, the carry. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.

**Fallbacks:**
- **Two-hand kettlebell swing:** Light two-hand KB deadlift with a fast stand, same sets × 3; technique fallback, not a ballistic equivalent.
- **Dumbbell/kettlebell reverse lunge:** Split-stance static lunge with hand support, same dose.
- **Knee-supported short-lever Copenhagen:** Side-lying adductor lift with the top leg supported, same sets and reps.
- **Standing barbell or Smith calf raise on a plate:** Standing calf-raise machine, same dose.
- **Rope pushdown:** Straight-bar or V-bar pushdown, same dose.
- **Pallof press:** Band Pallof press anchored to a rack, same dose.

**Load rules:** All main-lift loads are conditional. If the first work set is about 1 RIR harder than prescribed: OHP/dip/pull-up −2.5 lb, bench/squat −5 lb, deadlift −10 lb for the remaining sets; if ≥2 RIR harder, double that and drop the last back-off. Progress only after the previous comparable session hit its target RIR with normal technique, bar speed and tissue response. Never catch up a missed increase; change one variable at a time. Dips and pull-ups: log added load AND total system load (morning bodyweight + added). Examples use 170 lb; use your real weight.

**Rest:** OHP, dip, pull-up and bench work sets 2:00 minimum, up to 3:00 when reps or bar speed would suffer (clock budgets 2:30). Squat/deadlift 2:30–3:00 (clock budgets 3:00). Power 60–90 s. Accessories 60–75 s. Supersets pair unrelated muscles and never include priority work sets.

**Power rules:** Power is fast and low-fatigue, never a conditioning circuit. Stop a set at the first visibly slower rep, a noisy catch or landing, or any technique breakdown. After TWO crisp, symptom-free exposures that did not slow the next main-lift ramp, change ONE thing: the next load step, or (only if load cannot rise) a little more height/distance. Never progress power in the same week as a new plyo stage or a new leg-accessory load. W6/W12 use half the sets at a familiar load. Power sets never count as strength sets.

**Power progression (Two-hand kettlebell swing):** After two crisp exposures: next bell size (4 kg step). Weeks 7–11 may move to the one-arm snatch if its gate is met.

**Arm rules:** Arms are for size: 3×8–15 at 1–2 RIR, last set may reach 0–1 RIR; 60–90 s before the same exercise comes round again. Add reps within 8–15 at the next normal appearance of the SAME exercise. When all 3 sets reach 15 at target reserve, add the smallest load step next time and return toward 8–10. Hold triceps first if Sunday dips or Wednesday OHP ramps feel slow or elbows are sore. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR, no progression.

**Calf rules:** Calves: full range, 1-s pause in the stretched position, no bouncing, 3 RIR (week 1: 3–4 RIR while choosing loads). When every set reaches the top of the range with normal next-morning calf/Achilles response AND normal Wednesday pogo quality, add the smallest load step. More than mild calf soreness on Wednesday morning: hold calf load and start Wednesday impact at the lower pogo dose. W6/W12: 2 easy sets at ≥4 RIR.

**Leg rules:** Extra leg work is never cut: under fatigue lighten the load, shorten the range or add hand support instead. Two work sets per side, 3–4 RIR. Increase the smallest load step only after two comparable exposures at ≥3 RIR with normal adductor/hamstring response and unaffected rides, impact and main lifts. Do not raise it in a week that impact or power progresses. W6/W12: 2×4/side at ≥5 RIR, lighter.

**Core rules:** Keep each core exercise for its three-week block. Improve range or hold quality first; after two good exposures take the smallest load/range step, without adding sets. No swinging on hanging raises (bent knees are the regression). Hollow holds end with ~10 s of good position left. Pallof is anti-rotation, not dynamic rotation. W6/W12: 2 easy sets.

**Sequencing:** Monday checks: swings/snatches come before deadlifts — if the deadlift ramps feel slow, stop power at 2 sets next time. Lunges come after a Sunday long ride and deadlifts — after a hard ride weekend, hold the lunge load.

**Cuts:** Cut order: (1) power sets 3–4, (2) 3rd triceps set, (3) 3rd calf set. Never cut: pull-up, deadlift and OHP work sets, both lunge sets, Copenhagen, both Pallof sets. No exercise is ever done for just 1 set.

**Fatigue and changes:** Fatigue levels: 0 = progress or hold normally. 1 = hold loads, rest toward 3:00, drop cut-first sets. 2 = cut the affected domain’s work sets by 25–35%, keep familiar technique work. 3 = 5–7 days at about half volume, 10–15% lighter, ≥4 RIR, no fast sprints or high-tier plyo, recommend easy or skipped rides. One bad session is not a deload. A tissue-safety stop happens immediately. Allowed without asking: small load changes, holds, one back-off set, small rep changes, longer rest, removing cut-first work. Needs Brian’s approval: replacing a primary lift or changing its frequency, >15% volume change, new deload/test changes, faster or higher-impact progression, priority or OHP-target changes. Weeks 1–4: present coaching changes for approval.

**Log:** sets/reps/load (and system load for dips/pull-ups), RIR, technique, bar speed (crisp/normal/grindy) on the last priority set; power load and quality; actual strength and impact time; contacts and sprint metres; next-morning adductor/calf/Achilles check; morning bodyweight; rides completed.

**Strength clock** (every block counted once; m:ss):
| Start–end | Block | What happens | Seconds |
|---|---|---|---|
| 0:00–5:00 | Warm-up | Thoracic rotations 6/side; Gentle hip rock-back 5/side; Bodyweight squat 5; Wall slides 8; Brace rehearsal 3 × 5 s; KB complex primer 2 untimed rounds: 2 double-KB cleans + 1 press + 2 front squats, light 8–12 kg bells, ~30 s between rounds. General prep (walk, ankle rocks, calf raises, marching, quarter-squats) was done on the impact clock and is not repeated. If impact was skipped, add a 60 s walk and 8 ankle rocks/side from the delay reserve. Preparation only: no work-set or accessory credit. Wall slides live here and nowhere else. | 220 execution + 45 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 20 rounding = 300 |
| 5:00–10:30 | Daily power | The KB complex in the warm-up is the rehearsal. 60 s between sets; 45 s before the next ramps. | 36 execution + 180 rest + 30 setup and load changes + 20 transition + 45 recovery before next + 19 rounding = 330 |
| 10:30–18:00 | Squat ramps | 45×5 → 30 s; 135×3 → 60 s; 185×1 → 90 s; 205×1 → 120 s. Each rest follows that ramp, including the last rest before the first work set. Ramps never count as work sets. | 52 execution + 300 rest + 75 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 8 rounding = 450 |
| 18:00–25:30 | Squat work (maintenance) | Full rest between every work set (2:00–3:00 upper, 2:30–3:00 lower). No accessory work during priority rests. | 45 execution + 360 rest + 20 transition + 25 rounding = 450 |
| 25:30–29:00 | Dip ramps | BW×3 → 45 s; +15×1 → 75 s. Each rest follows that ramp, including the last rest before the first work set. Ramps never count as work sets. | 22 execution + 120 rest + 45 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 8 rounding = 210 |
| 29:00–36:00 | Dip work | Full rest between every work set (2:00–3:00 upper, 2:30–3:00 lower). No accessory work during priority rests. | 72 execution + 300 rest + 20 transition + 28 rounding = 420 |

| 40:00–43:30 | Pull-up work | Full rest between every work set (2:00–3:00 upper, 2:30–3:00 lower). No accessory work during priority rests. | 30 execution + 150 rest + 20 transition + 10 rounding = 210 |
| 43:30–49:30 | Single-leg RDL + DB incline bench (superset) | Round = single-leg RDL (both sides), then a DB incline set; repeat. A single-leg hinge and a chest press use different muscles, so they share rest instead of each needing its own recovery. No extra rest needed: each exercise rests while its partner works (≥60 s). | 176 execution + 20 side changes + 45 station moves + 45 setup and load changes + 15 transition + 30 rehearsal + 15 rehearsal rest + 14 rounding = 360 |
